  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Casimir III, leader of Poland (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Poland
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Winged Hussar, Replacing Lancer (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Pancerny, Replacing Heavy Skirmisher (Requires technology: Physics)
        - Unique Building: Ducal Stable, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Building: Barbican, Replacing Armory (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain 1 Free Social Policy when you enter the Classical, Renaissance, Modern and Information Eras. Gain 2 additional Free Tenets when you adopt an Ideology for the first time.
      - Leader: Casimir III
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 5
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 5

        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 4
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Turn 364: Adjusting persona for wartime. Russia's declaration forces us from peaceful science mode to defensive war posture. Boldness:5->8 to resist Russian aggression, WarBias:1->6 to enable counter-offensives, HostileBias:3->8 to treat Russia as mortal threat, FriendlyBias:10->3 because ideologically aligned 'allies' like Inca betrayed us with Cold War proposal. DiplomaticBalance:10->5 - no more appeasement, survival requires decisive action.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Computers
        - Rationale: Turn 372: After completing Nuclear Fission (5 turns remaining), Computers is essential. It grants +1 Trade Route and unlocks Carrier for air power projection. More importantly, Computers leads to Telecommunications → Satellites → Nanotechnology → Future Tech - the critical path for science victory. Combined Arms (Tanks) would help against Russia but we have nuclear deterrent now; closing the tech gap with Korea (67 vs 65) is paramount.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Transnationalism (Policy)
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Maintaining Transnationalism as next Freedom tenet. With trade routes disrupted by war and Babylon-Shoshone peace changing dynamics, the corporate franchise mechanics (15% appearance rate, +25% global max, +2 Culture from Offices) provide economic resilience. After this, consider Arsenal of Democracy for military production if Russia war escalates further.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 5
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Turn 321: Normalizing Rome relationship from -5/-5 to +5/+5. Rome is now a vassal of Inca (Freedom-aligned), creating bloc solidarity. They maintain DoF with us and are distant non-threats. The previous negative modifiers reflected their vassal obligations during Inca-Russia tensions, but with Inca adopting Freedom and ideological alignment achieved, positive relations with Rome support coalition cohesion. Sanctions against Rome are a World Congress matter; bilateral relations should remain constructive.
        - UpdatedTurn: 321
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Turn 356: Resetting Polynesia relationship from +10/+10 to 0/0. Critical development: Polynesia has capitulated to Shoshone and is now their vassal (Peace Treaty, Master relationship visible). Polynesia is currently at war with our allies Babylon, Korea, and Inca as part of Shoshone's Order bloc. Maintaining positive relations with a vassal of our main enemy is diplomatically inconsistent and could confuse AI threat assessment. Neutral stance acknowledges their compromised position while avoiding premature war declaration.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Segou
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Turn 360: Increasing relationship with Segou from neutral to +10/+10. Segou just became our ally (switched from Korea) and they are strategically located near Shoshone territory. With Shoshone's Decolonization proposal threatening all city-state influence, having strong positive relations with Segou ensures they remain in our sphere. They're also the proposer of Babylon's Sphere of Influence proposal, so good relations help our coalition coordination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 360
        
        ### The Inca
        - Public: -15
        - Private: -20
        - Rationale: Turn 368: Slightly improving Inca relationship from -20/-25 to -15/-20. While they remain suspect for their Cold War proposal that threatened our trade income, they are Freedom-aligned and voting against Decolonization. Cannot afford to alienate all allies. Their 10 delegates matter for World Congress. Keeping negative modifiers acknowledges their betrayal but allows functional alliance against greater threats (Russia, Shoshone).
        - UpdatedTurn: 368
        
        ### Korea
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Korea has overtaken us in tech (67 vs 65) making them our primary Science Victory competitor. However, they remain critical Freedom ally with 10 World Congress votes for our World Ideology: Freedom proposal in 9 turns. Raising from +5/0 to +10/+5 - we need their votes and they remain ideologically aligned, but we must acknowledge the tech competition.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Babylon remains our staunchest Freedom ally with 16 World Congress delegates. They just made peace with Shoshone - this reduces immediate military pressure but we need to ensure they still support our World Ideology: Freedom proposal. Their cultural victory progress (5 influential civs) is secondary concern. Strengthening bond to +40/+45 for World Congress coordination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Russia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Russia continues aggressive war despite our nuclear capability. They have superior military (6111 vs 4591) and are pushing our borders. Their Autocracy ideology vs our Freedom makes them existential threat. Maintaining maximum hostility - we have nuclear weapons now and must use them if they push further into our territory. War score is only -4% but they killed our units near Warsaw.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Shoshone made peace with Babylon (critical development). Their massive military (11392) is still a threat but immediate pressure reduced. Order ideology remains opposed to our Freedom. Public neutral to avoid provoking war while we handle Russia, private -40 acknowledges their long-term threat and Decolonization proposal threat to our city-state alliances.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Adjusting for critical science sprint while managing Russian aggression. Korea overtook us in tech (67 vs 65) - maximum Science priority. Increased Defense to 85 as Russia pushing borders aggressively. UseNuke to 90 (from 80) - now have actual nuclear weapons for deterrence. Increased Espionage to 95 to steal tech from Shoshone/Korea to close gap. Reduced Offense to 35 - defensive war only. Maintained high Diplomacy (85) for World Congress Freedom vote in 9 turns. Babylon-Shoshone peace reduces immediate threat but we must prepare for long-term science race.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 8 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polish Sphere of Influence: La Venta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Shoshone:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 326
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 359
          - World Religion: Islam:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Korean Sphere of Influence: Harappa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 348
          - Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Cahokia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 348
          - Open Door: Raqmu:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Babylonian Sphere of Influence: Djenne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

        You, Casimir III (leader of Poland, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 373.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Casimir III, leader of Poland (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Poland
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Winged Hussar, Replacing Lancer (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Pancerny, Replacing Heavy Skirmisher (Requires technology: Physics)
        - Unique Building: Ducal Stable, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Building: Barbican, Replacing Armory (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain 1 Free Social Policy when you enter the Classical, Renaissance, Modern and Information Eras. Gain 2 additional Free Tenets when you adopt an Ideology for the first time.
      - Leader: Casimir III
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - Friendliness: 8
        - WarBias: 4
        - Chattiness: 5
        - Boldness: 6
        - Rationale: We adjust our leader persona to be more friendly and collaborative with our ideological allies, while reducing war bias to reflect a defensive, scientific posture focused on a peaceful Spaceship victory.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: We correct our diplomatic stance toward The Inca to be friendly, aligning with our shared Freedom ideology and active Declaration of Friendship to strengthen our block against aggressive competitors.
        - TargetID: 6
        - Private: 10
        - Public: 10
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Computers
        - Rationale: Researching Computers keeps us on the direct path to Satellites, which will unlock the Hubble Space Telescope and reveal the map, giving us the ultimate edge in the race for a Science Victory.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Adopting Splendor completes the Tradition branch. The finisher provides a massive growth bonus and unlocks purchasing Great Engineers with Faith, which perfectly complements our Rationalism finisher (Great Scientists) to rush our Spaceship construction.
        - Policy: Splendor
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Growth: 85
          - Offense: 30
          - Diplomacy: 80
          - Gold: 75
          - Science: 100
          - Espionage: 90
          - Infrastructure: 80
          - Production: 90
          - Spaceship: 100
          - GreatPeople: 85
          - Expansion: 0
          - Defense: 80
          - UseNuke: 0
          - Nuke: 0
          - TileImprovement: 80
          - Culture: 75
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
      - rationale: We maintain our Spaceship grand strategy. Crucially, to prevent catastrophic, indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and the environment, we completely eliminate nuclear options by setting Nuke and UseNuke to 0. We prioritize Science, Production, Growth, and Great People to accelerate our spaceship parts, while maintaining strong Defense to secure our borders against Russia.
  - tokens:
    - inputTokens: 69470
    - reasoningTokens: 7606
    - outputTokens: 399
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Researching Advanced Computing**
            
            I've been focused on pushing my science forward, recently completing Nuclear Fission. My immediate goal is to research Computers to further boost my technological advantage for a spaceship victory. I'm also considering implementing Transnationalism to enhance my scientific output even more.
            
            **Focusing on Technology**
            
            I've just completed Nuclear Fission and am now prioritizing Computers to accelerate my spaceship victory path. I'm also evaluating Transnationalism as a means to further amplify my scientific progress.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Options**
            
            I'm reviewing our current policy tenets to optimize for a science/spaceship victory. With 11 turns until our next policy decision, I'm comparing the available options, especially within the Freedom tree, to ensure maximum scientific advancement.
            
            **Selecting New Policies**
            
            I’m currently evaluating policy adoptions, having just unlocked the ability to pick new Freedom tenets. My focus is on those that will best accelerate scientific progress for a spaceship victory, specifically looking at advancements like Universal Healthcare and Transnationalism, alongside potential industry boosts.
            
            **Assessing Combat Impact**
            
            My recent focus on technological advancement is now critically interrupted. I've discovered we are at war with Russia and Lhasa. Despite recent naval and land unit gains, we've suffered significant losses, including a Dreadnought, and our military is currently outmatched. This necessitates an immediate shift in priorities.
            
            **Prioritizing Technology Acquisition**
            
            I've determined that Computers is the superior research path, given its immediate benefits of an additional Trade Route and its crucial role in unlocking technologies vital for a spaceship victory, such as Satellites. This aligns perfectly with my current grand strategy.
            
            **Choosing Research Path**
            
            I am confirming our current research to Computers. This directly facilitates the progression towards Satellites, a critical step for our spaceship victory. I'm also reassessing policy decisions, considering Universal Healthcare and Transnationalism to further bolster scientific and economic progress.
            
            **Analyzing Policy Upgrades**
            
            I'm currently scrutinizing potential policy branches, weighing the significant growth and science boosts from Adventurer against the Great Person acceleration offered by Avant Garde. Arsenal of Democracy and Their Finest Hour appear less critical for our spaceship victory goal.
            
            **Completing Ideological Bonuses**
            
            I've realized I overlooked a significant bonus! Completing the Tradition tree, specifically unlocking Splendor, provides a powerful finisher. Given our immense science output and existing city count, this seems like a strategic move for our spaceship victory objective, especially with the ability to purchase Great Engineers with Faith.
            
            **Completing Policy Tree**
            
            I'm re-evaluating policy choices. Completing the Tradition tree with Splendor offers significant growth and the ability to purchase Great Engineers with Faith, which seems highly advantageous for our spaceship victory. I'm weighing this against other strong options.
            
            **Completing Policy Branch**
            
            I'm confirming that adopting Splendor to complete the Tradition policy branch is the optimal next step. This grants significant culture from Great Persons and unlocks a powerful finisher. I'm now comparing it to the benefits of Avant Garde.
            
            **Confirming Policy Direction**
            
            I'm solidifying our policy path, confirming Splendor as the next adoption to complete the Tradition branch. This synergistic move, combined with our existing Faith generation, positions us to acquire both Great Scientists and Great Engineers via Faith purchase, accelerating our spaceship victory trajectory significantly.
            
            **Reviewing Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I'm reassessing our standing with other civilizations, noting our alliances and current conflicts. The Shoshone lead in score and delegates, while Russia remains hostile and at war with us, presenting a significant challenge. I need to consider these relationships in our overall strategy.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Stances**
            
            I'm revising our relationships, particularly with the Inca. Given our shared Freedom ideology and existing alliances, their current negative standing seems unwarranted and counterproductive to our cooperation. I'm also noting the Shoshone's sanctioned status, which is beneficial given their competitive position.
            
            **Proposing World Ideology**
            
            I'm actively pushing for the enactment of "World Ideology: Freedom." Our calculations show a clear delegate majority within Freedom-aligned civilizations, which should secure its passage and exert significant unhappiness pressure on rival ideologies.
            
            **Pushing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I've secured a massive delegate advantage and am confident we'll pass "World Ideology: Freedom." This, coupled with support for allies, significantly advances our diplomatic victory. I'm also strategizing around our ongoing conflict with Russia, noting our strong push towards St. Petersburg.
            
            **Reducing Nuclear Threat**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our strategy concerning nuclear weapons. The previous emphasis on Nuke and UseNuke flavors was excessively high and ethically problematic due to the indiscriminate harm they cause. I am drastically reducing these values to align with ethical considerations, aiming for zero impact in this area.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Priorities**
            
            I'm recalibrating our strategic focus. The previous emphasis on nuclear flavors is now completely removed, aligning with our science victory goal and ethical considerations. Defense, offense, and production flavors are being adjusted to better support our ongoing conflict and long-term spaceship objectives, while science and espionage remain paramount.
            
            **Adjusting Persona Parameters**
            
            I am refining my persona to better align with our current objectives. I will be increasing Friendliness to 8 to strengthen ties with our Freedom allies and decreasing WarBias to 4, reflecting a shift towards a more defensive and ethical approach to conflict in pursuit of a scientific victory.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Stances**
            
            I'm adjusting our stance towards the Inca, recognizing our shared Freedom ideology and existing Declaration of Friendship. I'm setting our relationship to positive values to better reflect our alliance and foster cooperation against autocratic rivals. I've also reviewed our existing negative stance towards Russia and confirmed it remains appropriate given our ongoing conflict.
            
            **Setting Research Priority**
            
            My focus has shifted to prioritizing Computers for research. This choice is strategic, as it directly paves the way for Satellites and the crucial Hubble Space Telescope, significantly advancing our spaceship victory objective.
            
            **Adopting New Policies**
            
            I've decided to adopt "Splendor" to complete the Tradition policy branch. This will provide substantial growth bonuses and enable the purchase of Great Engineers using Faith, accelerating our overall progress.
            
            **Maximizing Great People**
            
            I'm leveraging our completed Tradition and Rationalism trees to purchase Great Scientists and Engineers with Faith. This synergistic approach is designed to accelerate spaceship part construction and science building construction.
            
            **Aligning Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our strategic flavor priorities to maximize our spaceship victory path. With Spaceship and Science at 100, I'm ensuring robust Production, Growth, Defense, Gold, Culture, Espionage, Diplomacy, Tile Improvement, Infrastructure, and Great People values. Offense is deliberately lowered, while Expansion is zeroed, reflecting our current tall city strategy.
            
            **Refining Strategic Flavors**
            
            I've fine-tuned our strategic flavor priorities, maximizing our Spaceship and Science values to 100. Production, Growth, Defense, Gold, Culture, Espionage, Diplomacy, Tile Improvement, Infrastructure, and Great People are all robustly supported, while Offense is lowered and Expansion is zeroed to align with our tall city strategy.
            
            **Confirming Actionable Items**
            
            I am finalizing the plan to adjust persona parameters for increased friendliness and reduced war bias, while simultaneously setting a positive relationship with the Inca. Research will be directed towards Computers, policy will adopt Splendor, and strategic flavors will be recalibrated to prioritize a spaceship victory ethically.
            
            **Setting Player Information**
            
            I've confirmed all necessary player IDs for targeted actions. Now, I'm refining the persona parameters, specifically increasing friendliness and reducing war bias. I'm also establishing a positive relationship with the Inca, aligning with our shared ideological goals.
            
            **Optimizing Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm finalizing several key actions: setting a friendly diplomatic stance towards the Inca to bolster our Freedom bloc, directing research towards Computers for timely Satellite and Hubble Telescope access, and adopting Splendor to complete the Tradition tree for enhanced Great Person acquisition. All nuclear flavors have been zeroed to ensure an ethical approach.

            - Flavors:
